People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Um, hi. Thanks for seeing me. I guess… I came in because people have started noticing that I’m kind of moving differently. Like sometimes I’m really slowed down, like I’m moving underwater or taking forever to answer someone, or even just get up from a chair.

And then other times I’m sort of the opposite—pacing around my apartment, rubbing my hands like I’m trying to shake off a bad call or something. I know that probably sounds a little odd, but it’s been noticeable enough that I thought I should get it checked out.

Yeah… I think so. When I’m moving really slowly, it usually lines up with the really low stretches. Like, I’m not eating much or sleeping right, and I feel guilty about… honestly, almost everything.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n2\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF31.9, F42.2, G24.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, hi. Thanks for seeing me. I guess… I came in because people have started noticing that I’m kind of moving differently. Like sometimes I’m really slowed down, like I’m moving underwater or taking forever to answer someone, or even just get up from a chair.\n\nAnd then other times I’m sort of the opposite—pacing around my apartment, rubbing my hands like I’m trying to shake off a bad call or something. I know that probably sounds a little odd, but it’s been noticeable enough that I thought I should get it checked out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood when you're moving slowly or quickly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m moving really slowly, it usually lines up with the really low stretches. When I’m moving really slowly, it usually lines up with the really low stretches. Like, I’m not eating much or sleeping right, and I feel guilty about… honestly, almost everything.\n\nAnd, um—I don’t want this to sound dramatic or like I’m making a big deal out of it—but during the worst of that, I also start hearing these headset beeps, like from my old customer service job. And I get kind of stuck on this thought that the system from there is still… tracking me somehow. I know that probably sounds strange. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe the system from your old job is still tracking you, or is that just a thought that you can't shake?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know how it sounds, and part of me really wants to say it’s just a thought. But if I’m being honest, yeah, sometimes I do believe it.\n\nEven when I’m having a pretty okay week, I’ll get this strong feeling that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow following me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. I know that probably doesn’t make sense from the outside… but in the moment it feels real enough that I start acting like it’s true.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience periods where you feel extremely happy or irritable and have a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not really “extremely happy,” no. I mean, I can get irritable sometimes, but it feels more like stress or being on edge than having a ton of energy or feeling amazing.\n\nI’m not sure if I’m answering that the right way. I haven’t really noticed periods where I’m suddenly super energized or unusually upbeat.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic and irritable at the same time?\n\nPatient: Not that I can really point to, no. I know that probably sounds a little odd, but it’s been noticeable enough that I thought I should get it checked out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood when you're moving slowly or quickly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m moving really slowly, it usually lines up with the really low stretches. Like, I’m not eating much or sleeping right, and I feel guilty about… honestly, almost everything.\n\nAnd, um—I don’t want this to sound dramatic or like I’m making a big deal out of it—but during the worst of that, I also start hearing these headset beeps, like from my old customer service job. And I get kind of stuck on this thought that the system from there is still… tracking me somehow. I know that probably sounds strange. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe the system from your old job is still tracking you, or is that just a thought that you can't shake?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know how it sounds, and part of me really wants to say it’s just a thought. But if I’m being honest, yeah, sometimes I do believe it.\n\nEven when I’m having a pretty okay week, I’ll get this strong feeling that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow following me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. I know that probably doesn’t make sense from the outside… but in the moment it feels real enough that I start acting like it’s true.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience periods where you feel extremely happy or irritable and have a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not really “extremely happy,” no. I mean, I can get irritable sometimes, but it feels more like stress or being on edge than having a ton of energy or feeling amazing.\n\nI’m not sure if I’m answering that the right way. I haven’t really noticed periods where I’m suddenly super energized or unusually upbeat.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic and irritable at the same time?\n\nPatient: Not that I can really point to, no. I mean, I’ve been irritable, sure, but it doesn’t come with feeling unusually energetic or like I’m running on extra fuel.\n\nI hope that makes sense… it’s more like being tense than being energized.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special powers or abilities that others don't have?\n\nPatient: Not special powers, no… I don’t think of it like that.\n\nIt’s more like… there are times I get really convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is still somehow tracking me through job sites, like it’s flagging me as unreliable. I know that probably sounds strange, and I’m not trying to say I have some ability other people don’t have. It’s just… even when I’m doing okay, that idea can feel really real to me. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you have these thoughts about the ticketing software tracking you all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go, I guess… but not completely. Like, even when I’m having an okay week, I can still get caught on the thought that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow following me through job sites and marking me as unreliable.\n\nI know that probably sounds… not great. I don’t mean it’s every second of the day, but it can pop up and feel pretty convincing. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people or system tracking you are trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think… harm exactly. I mean, I’m not trying to say there are people out to get me physically or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like I get convinced the system from my old job is still tracking me through job sites and sort of flagging me as unreliable, like it’s making it harder for me to get work. I know that probably sounds odd, but that’s usually the way it feels. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any times when you don't believe the system from your old job is tracking you at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, there are times when it’s quieter, I guess. Like I’m not actively thinking about it every minute.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, even when I’m having a decent week, I can still kind of get pulled back into believing the ticketing software from my old job is somehow following me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. I know that probably sounds irrational, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but it doesn’t fully go away for me. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to express your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. I mean, I’ve been irritable, sure, but it doesn’t come with feeling unusually energetic or like I’m running on extra fuel.\n\nI hope that makes sense… it’s more like being tense than being energized.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special powers or abilities that others don't have?\n\nPatient: Not special powers, no… I don’t think of it like that.\n\nIt’s more like… there are times I get really convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is still somehow tracking me through job sites, like it’s flagging me as unreliable. I know that probably sounds strange, and I’m not trying to say I have some ability other people don’t have. It’s just… even when I’m doing okay, that idea can feel really real to me. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you have these thoughts about the ticketing software tracking you all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go, I guess… but not completely. Like, even when I’m having an okay week, I can still get caught on the thought that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow following me through job sites and marking me as unreliable.\n\nI know that probably sounds… not great. I don’t mean it’s every second of the day, but it can pop up and feel pretty convincing. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people or system tracking you are trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think… harm exactly. I mean, I’m not trying to say there are people out to get me physically or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like I get convinced the system from my old job is still tracking me through job sites and sort of flagging me as unreliable, like it’s making it harder for me to get work. I know that probably sounds odd, but that’s usually the way it feels. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any times when you don't believe the system from your old job is tracking you at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, there are times when it’s quieter, I guess. I know that probably sounds irrational, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but it doesn’t fully go away for me. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to express your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your thoughts about the ticketing software tracking you something that you think about every day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m sitting with it every minute of every day, but… yeah, it’s probably there most days in some way.\n\nEven when I’m having an okay week, if I’m on job sites or thinking about applying somewhere, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me and flagging me as unreliable. Yeah… I think job sites are probably the biggest trigger. Like if I’m looking at postings or filling something out, that’s when it gets louder in my head.

Even on a pretty okay week, I can start feeling convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow following me through those sites and marking me as unreliable. I know that probably doesn’t sound realistic, and I’m not trying to make it seem dramatic… but that’s usually when it catches me. Is that the kind of trigger you mean?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
A little, yeah… though I don’t know if it’s exactly trouble finding the words.

People close to me have said I sound kind of flat now, or hard to read, even when I’m actually listening and I do care about what they’re saying. I guess I tend to give shorter answers than I mean to, and then I avoid making plans because it feels like I won’t come across right. I don’t mean to seem uninterested.

I mean, I’ve been irritable, sure, but it doesn’t come with feeling unusually energetic or like I’m running on extra fuel.\n\nI hope that makes sense… it’s more like being tense than being energized.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special powers or abilities that others don't have?\n\nPatient: Not special powers, no… I don’t think of it like that.\n\nIt’s more like… there are times I get really convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is still somehow tracking me through job sites, like it’s flagging me as unreliable. I don’t mean it’s every second of the day, but it can pop up and feel pretty convincing. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you completely don't believe the system from your old job is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Completely? Um… not really, no. Like, I’m not eating much or sleeping right, and I feel guilty about… honestly, almost everything.\n\nAnd, um—I don’t want this to sound dramatic or like I’m making a big deal out of it—but during the worst of that, I also start hearing these headset beeps, like from my old customer service job. And I get kind of stuck on this thought that the system from there is still… tracking me somehow. I know that probably sounds strange. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe the system from your old job is still tracking you, or is that just a thought that you can't shake?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know how it sounds, and part of me really wants to say it’s just a thought. But if I’m being honest, yeah, sometimes I do believe it.\n\nEven when I’m having a pretty okay week, I’ll get this strong feeling that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow following me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. I know that probably doesn’t make sense from the outside… but in the moment it feels real enough that I start acting like it’s true.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience periods where you feel extremely happy or irritable and have a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not really “extremely happy,” no. I mean, I can get irritable sometimes, but it feels more like stress or being on edge than having a ton of energy or feeling amazing.\n\nI’m not sure if I’m answering that the right way. I haven’t really noticed periods where I’m suddenly super energized or unusually upbeat.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic and irritable at the same time?\n\nPatient: Not that I can really point to, no. I mean, I’ve been irritable, sure, but it doesn’t come with feeling unusually energetic or like I’m running on extra fuel.\n\nI hope that makes sense… it’s more like being tense than being energized.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special powers or abilities that others don't have?\n\nPatient: Not special powers, no… I don’t think of it like that.\n\nIt’s more like… there are times I get really convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is still somehow tracking me through job sites, like it’s flagging me as unreliable. I know that probably sounds strange, and I’m not trying to say I have some ability other people don’t have. It’s just… even when I’m doing okay, that idea can feel really real to me. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you have these thoughts about the ticketing software tracking you all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go, I guess… but not completely. Like, even when I’m having an okay week, I can still get caught on the thought that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow following me through job sites and marking me as unreliable.\n\nI know that probably sounds… not great. I don’t mean it’s every second of the day, but it can pop up and feel pretty convincing. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people or system tracking you are trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think… harm exactly. I mean, I’m not trying to say there are people out to get me physically or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like I get convinced the system from my old job is still tracking me through job sites and sort of flagging me as unreliable, like it’s making it harder for me to get work. I know that probably sounds odd, but that’s usually the way it feels. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any times when you don't believe the system from your old job is tracking you at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, there are times when it’s quieter, I guess. Like I’m not actively thinking about it every minute.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, even when I’m having a decent week, I can still kind of get pulled back into believing the ticketing software from my old job is somehow following me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. I know that probably sounds irrational, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but it doesn’t fully go away for me. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to express your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. I mean, sometimes I probably over-explain or get nervous that I’m not saying it right, but I haven’t really noticed people telling me I don’t make sense.\n\nIf anything, I worry about it after I talk, like, “Did that come out okay?” But I can usually get my point across, I think. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you completely don't believe the system from your old job is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Completely? Um… not really, no. I wish I could say there are times where I’m totally sure it’s not happening, but even on okay weeks it can still come back.\n\nLike, I’ll be applying for jobs and then I get convinced the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me through those sites and flagging me as unreliable. I know that probably sounds… kind of far-fetched, and I’m not trying to exaggerate it. It’s just hard for me to fully not believe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble staying on topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Not really, I don’t think so. I mean, I can ramble a little if I’m anxious or trying to explain myself, but I don’t usually have people say I’m jumping around or they can’t follow me.\n\nI try to stay on the question. If I go off a bit, I can usually notice and come back. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Are there any moments when you are completely free from the thought that the ticketing software is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… I mean, there are times it’s quieter and I can get through the day without focusing on it as much. But even on an okay week, it can still pop back in.\n\nLike if I’m on job sites, I’ll start thinking the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me there and marking me as unreliable. I know that probably sounds strange, and I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is… but I don’t really have moments where I’m totally free of the thought. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your thoughts about the ticketing software tracking you something that you think about every day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m sitting with it every minute of every day, but… yeah, it’s probably there most days in some way.\n\nEven when I’m having an okay week, if I’m on job sites or thinking about applying somewhere, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me and flagging me as unreliable. I know that sounds like a lot, and I don’t want to overstate it, but it does come up pretty regularly. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that the ticketing software is tracking you is something that might be false?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to tell myself it might be false, because I know it probably sounds unlikely when I say it out loud.\n\nBut when it’s happening, it feels pretty real to me. I mean, sometimes I probably over-explain or get nervous that I’m not saying it right, but I haven’t really noticed people telling me I don’t make sense.\n\nIf anything, I worry about it after I talk, like, “Did that come out okay?” But I can usually get my point across, I think. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you completely don't believe the system from your old job is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Completely? Um… not really, no. I wish I could say there are times where I’m totally sure it’s not happening, but even on okay weeks it can still come back.\n\nLike, I’ll be applying for jobs and then I get convinced the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me through those sites and flagging me as unreliable. I know that probably sounds… kind of far-fetched, and I’m not trying to exaggerate it. It’s just hard for me to fully not believe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble staying on topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Not really, I don’t think so. I mean, I can ramble a little if I’m anxious or trying to explain myself, but I don’t usually have people say I’m jumping around or they can’t follow me.\n\nI try to stay on the question. If I go off a bit, I can usually notice and come back. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Are there any moments when you are completely free from the thought that the ticketing software is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… I mean, there are times it’s quieter and I can get through the day without focusing on it as much. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your thoughts about the ticketing software tracking you something that you think about every day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m sitting with it every minute of every day, but… yeah, it’s probably there most days in some way.\n\nEven when I’m having an okay week, if I’m on job sites or thinking about applying somewhere, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me and flagging me as unreliable. I know that sounds like a lot, and I don’t want to overstate it, but it does come up pretty regularly. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that the ticketing software is tracking you is something that might be false?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to tell myself it might be false, because I know it probably sounds unlikely when I say it out loud.\n\nBut when it’s happening, it feels pretty real to me. Even on an okay week, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. I know that might not make logical sense, and I don’t want to sound like I’m insisting on it… but it’s hard for me to fully believe it’s not happening. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific triggers that make you more likely to believe the ticketing software is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think job sites are probably the biggest trigger. Like if I’m looking at postings or filling something out, that’s when it gets louder in my head.\n\nEven on a pretty okay week, I can start feeling convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow following me through those sites and marking me as unreliable. I know that probably doesn’t sound realistic, and I’m not trying to make it seem dramatic… but that’s usually when it catches me. I guess I tend to give shorter answers than I mean to, and then I avoid making plans because it feels like I won’t come across right. I don’t mean to seem uninterested.\n\nIs that what you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are there any times when you don't think about the ticketing software tracking you for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah… I mean, I’ve had stretches where I’m doing better for a few weeks, like my mood is more normal and I’m actually getting things done.\n\nBut I don’t think it fully goes away during those times. I can still hear the supervisor voice sometimes, or feel pretty sure the old ticketing software is flagging me. It’s just… maybe I handle it a little better, or I don’t talk about it as much. I’m not sure if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Are there any times when you are completely free from the thought that the ticketing software is tracking you for at least a week?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can honestly say a full week, no.
